The ASRock B860 Challenger Wifi is a solid $149 budget motherboard that handles our Intel Core Ultra 270K Plus without issue, but its USB is capped at 5 Gbps, sports weaker power delivery, and its BIOS is behind the times.

Why you can trust Tom's Hardware Our expert reviewers spend hours testing and comparing products and services so you can choose the best for you. Find out more about how we test.

Based on the mainstream B860 chipset, the ASRock B860 Challenger Wifi sits on the budget side of Intel's motherboard lineup, and costs a reasonable $149.99. You get a full-sized motherboard with full support for Intel’s Core Ultra and Core Ultra Series 2 (Arrow Lake and Arrow Lake refresh) processors. Still, some sacrifices in specs and features are inevitable at this price point.The all-black B860 Challenger (there is a white version, too) includes all the basics. You get eight rear USB ports, three M.2 sockets (one PCIe 5.0), four SATA ports, 2.5 GbE, Wi-Fi 7, and a basic audio solution. It falls a bit short on USB speed (5 Gbps max.), power delivery isn’t strong (though it handled our 270K Plus without issue at stock), and ASRock’s BIOS is fully functional but dated compared to the competition. It has everything that most will need from a motherboard, but it’s not the fastest or the best at anything, which you should generally expect from a low-priced mainstream chipset. Performance on this board was actually quite good in our testing. I have to admit, that surprised me a little given the VRM configuration. I expected it to throttle on some longer-running multi-threaded benchmarks. But the B860 Challenger proved me wrong and was average to above average across most of our tests. Whether gaming, doing office work, or juggling creative workflows, the Challenger handled our Intel Core Ultra 270K Plus without issue, though the VRMs ran hotter than on other tested boards. I would suggest using a chassis with good airflow with this board, especially if you plan to run a high-end processor and long-running, heavily multi-threaded applications.Below, we’ll examine the B860 Challenger's tested performance and other features to see if it earns a spot on our list of the best motherboards. But before we share test results and discuss details, here are the specifications from the ASRock website:

14 Phase (10x 50A Dr.MOS MOSFETs for Vcore)

(1) USB 3.2 Gen 1 (5 Gbps) Type-C(6) USB 3.2 Gen 1 (5 Gbps)

(1) PCIe 5.0 x4 (128 Gbps) / PCIe (up to 80mm)(2) PCIe 4.0 x4 (64 Gbps) / PCIe (up to 80mm)

(1) USB v3.2 Gen 1 (5 Gbps) Type-C(1) USB v3.2 Gen 1 (5 Gbps)(2) USB v2.0 (480 Mbps)

(3) 3-pin ARGB Gen2 headers(1) 4-pin RGB header

4-LED Post Status Checker (CPU, DRAM, VGA, Boot)

***Realtek 8922AE Wi-Fi 7 - 160 MHz, 6 GHz, BT 5.4

ASRock includes a few accessories to help get you up and running. You get two SATA cables, an M.2 screw and mount, Wi-Fi antennas, stickers, and a quick installation guide. The collection is sparse, but typical for a low-cost mainstream-class motherboard.

The B860 Challenger sits on a matte-black PCB with black connectors and heatsinks. Only the Challenger branding on a couple of heatsinks and the capacitors dot the board, breaking up the stealthy appearance. It lacks a ‘full coverage’ heatsink on the bottom, partly due to the M. 2's physical orientation, but two of the three M.2 sockets do have heatsinks. You won’t find any integrated RGB, but the board has multiple headers to add your own.

Overall, this is a plain-looking board with a lot of exposed PCB, but that's typical for this chipset and price. Still, it will blend well into any dark build theme.

In the top-left area of the board, we see the standard dual 8-pin EPS connectors to power the processor. Below that is the large VRM heatsink that extends to the rear IO area and covers some unsightly motherboard components. On top, we can see the silver Challenger branding. A second VRM heatsink covers the top portion of the power delivery. Both appeared to make good contact with the MOSFETs and inductors/chokes below.Moving right, past the socket, the next feature is the four DRAM slots, each with locking mechanisms on both sides. ASRock lists support for up to DDR5-8666, which is plenty fast for the platform, especially given today’s ridiculous memory prices.

Tucked between the heatsink and the DRAM slots on the left, along with two others on the right side of the slots, are the first (of seven) 4-pin fan headers. Per usual, each header supports PWM and DC-controlled devices. The CPU_FAN1 connector supports up to 1A/12W, while the remainder support up to 3A/36W. In other words, you won’t have any issues powering your system fans from the board. Just be sure not to overload them. You can control the headers via the BIOS or the A-Tuning app with FAN-Tastic tuning.

Rounding the corner and moving down the right edge, you'll find the first two 3-pin ARGB headers. Along the bottom edge are additional headers: a third ARGB header and a single 4-pin RGB header. The Polychrome Sync application in the BIOS or as a standalone app controls the attached devices. Next are the 24-pin ATX connector to power the board, a front-panel 19-pin USB 3.2 Gen1 (5 Gbps) header, and a front-panel USB 3.2 Gen1 (5 Gbps) Type-C header. Surprisingly, 5 Gbps is the fastest you’ll get out of any USB port on this board. For 2026, that’s disappointing, even in a budget-class mainstream board.

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

Power delivery on the B860 Challenger is paltry, but not by phase count; it’s 14 phases total, 10 dedicated to Vcore — but by the weak MOSFETs. Power heads from the EPS connectors to a Richtek RT3638AJ 3-rail, 8-phase PWM controller (meaning the Vcore runs ‘teamed,’ common in today’s systems). From there, power goes to 10 50A Vishay SiC632 Dr.MOS power stages. The 500A available isn’t a lot, but it handled our Core Ultra 270K Plus without throttling. That said, the VRMs ran hot during our 30-minute stress test, so make sure you have good airflow to keep temperatures down.

On the bottom half, starting on the left edge, is the audio solution: the basic Realtek ALC897 and a couple of dedicated audio capacitors. This isn’t the best audio section around, but par for this class of board. Still, most users will be satisfied with this implementation.Two full-length PCIe slots sit in the middle of the board and couldn’t be spaced further apart. The top slot is reinforced, connects through the CPU, and is your primary slot for graphics running PCIe 5.0 x16 speeds (it does not bifurcate). The bottom slot connects through the chipset and runs up to PCIe 4.0 x4. The large gap between them lets you fit any video card, while still leaving that bottom slot open for expansion.

Mixed in and around the PCIe slots are three M.2 sockets. The first, above the primary PCIe slot, uses a larger heatsink with a quick-release mechanism that makes installing and removing the device easy. Under the heatsink is a CPU-connected M.2, M2_1, that runs at PCIe 5.0 x4 (128 Gbps) and supports devices up to 80mm. The other two M.2 sockets run at PCIe 4.0 x4 (64 Gbps), with one, M2_2, under a simple (not EZ DIY) heatsink and the other, M2_3, with no heatsink. If you want to use RAID on the M.2 drives, you’ll have to look elsewhere, as the B860 Challenger doesn't list support for that here. Note that the primary M.2 socket made good contact with the M.2 drive. Moving right, past the chipset heatsink to the edge of the board, we reach the four SATA ports. These you can use RAID with (RAID0/1/5/10) for that extra speed or redundancy.Finally, we snapped a few images of the board’s active ICs. This ASRock board uses a combination of Richtek (PWM controller), ASMedia (USB), Realtek (audio and networking), and Vishay (MOSFETs).

At the bottom are several headers, including front-panel audio and USB ports. A complete list of connectivity is listed below, from left to right:

Flipping the board around to the rear I/O, we can see enough ports for most users. Starting on the left, we see the HDMI (v2.1) output for integrated graphics, with the Wi-Fi 7 (the ‘slower’ 160 Hz) antenna screw-in connections above it. Moving right, we spy the only USB Type-C port on the rear IO, and it’s a slow 5 Gbps port. In fact, 5 Gbps is the fastest USB you’ll see on this board, which will disappoint anyone looking for speedy USB.

Next are two USB 2.0 ports (black), six more 5 Gbps ports (blue and yellow: the latter are the Lightning USB ports for reduced latency). Above the yellow Lightning Gaming ports is the Realtek-based 2.5 GbE. Next are the audio inputs/outputs, with three 3.5mm jacks (input/output/microphone). The B860 Challenger lacks SPDIF optical output, which could affect users who want to send the audio signal to an AV/R or other external output.

If you’re an esports gamer where every millisecond counts, the Lightning Gaming ports could come in handy. Otherwise, the feature adds little value for the average user.

#10280. Google Photos update fixes the bug that hid edited pictures

A bug in Google Photos has left edited pictures invisible to other Android apps, but Google has now rolled out a fix.

Since roughly July/August of this year, editing a picture in Google Photos has left Android users with the bizarre side effect of making that image unavailable to other apps. After saving the edit, the picture would vanish from the image picker in apps like Telegram, WhatsApp, and more. A workaround to this was to save the image as a copy rather than the original, but it’s frustrating nonetheless.

Google has thankfully, finally fixed this issue.

The fix is apparently in Google Photos v7.97, Artem Russakovskii (owner of APKMirror) mentions, and we’ve been able to confirm the hidden image behavior no longer occurs on v7.98 either. The company acknowledged the problem last month and confirmed a fix would be coming.

The exact cause remains unclear at this point, but that doesn’t matter now seeing as the problem is fixed. If you’re still seeing the bug, let us know in the comments below.

#10275. Building CRM Automations That Don't Break: APIs, Webhooks, and AI Validation

Have 30-200 Employees? Make $50k-$500k selling your data for AI Training.

Have 30-200 Employees? Make $50k-$500k selling your data for AI Training.

Stay up to date with AI tools, model releases, and developer workflows that matter.

Modern businesses rarely struggle with a shortage of software. The bigger problem is getting their existing systems to communicate properly.

A lead arrives through a website form, but the sales team doesn't receive a notification. Someone books an appointment, yet their CRM record isn't updated. A prospect replies to an automated message, but the follow-up sequence continues as though nothing happened.

These are integration problems rather than software problems.

Platforms such as GoHighLevel aim to solve them by combining contact management, messaging, appointment scheduling and workflow automation in one environment. However, the real value comes from how these components are configured and connected to other applications.

For developers, agencies and technical consultants, GoHighLevel provides an interesting example of how low-code automation and API-driven development can work together.

This guide explores the architecture behind an effective CRM automation system, how GoHighLevel's integrations operate, and where custom development makes sense. The patterns below — data normalisation, idempotent event handling, queue-based processing and validated AI outputs — apply to any CRM; GoHighLevel is used here as the running example because it combines native workflows with a public API and webhooks in one platform.

It's tempting to think of automation as a collection of simple rules.

When someone submits a form, send an email. When a customer books a meeting, update their status. When a payment arrives, trigger a notification.

Individually, these are straightforward tasks.

Problems emerge when different workflows start interacting.

Consider a business that generates leads through several channels:

Referral partners and external lead providers

Each channel introduces different information, sometimes in different formats.

One source might supply a full name, phone number and email address. Another provides only a phone number. A third might include campaign information, product preferences or qualification answers.

Without a consistent data structure, CRM records become fragmented. Duplicate contacts appear, important information disappears and automations begin acting on incomplete data.

A well-designed system needs to address these problems before adding more workflow complexity.

GoHighLevel combines native workflow tools with an API that allows developers to integrate external services.

Its core components include contact records, conversations, calendars, opportunities, pipelines and workflow automation.

Developers can interact with supported resources through the platform's REST API, while webhooks provide a mechanism for reacting to events.

Information enters the system through forms, funnels, integrations, inbound communication or API requests.

At this stage, the priority is collecting reliable information and associating it with the correct contact.

Once information has been received, workflows can evaluate conditions and initiate actions.

For example, a newly created lead might trigger an internal notification, a qualification sequence or an appointment invitation.

Some business processes require functionality beyond the CRM's native capabilities.

An organisation might need to synchronise an accounting platform, update an external database or pass information into a proprietary application.

This is where APIs, webhooks and middleware become especially useful.

The important architectural decision is determining which tasks belong inside the CRM and which should be handled externally.

GoHighLevel's developer platform offers REST endpoints for supported operations involving contacts, conversations, calendars, payments and other CRM resources.

Its API uses JSON and supports token-based authentication, including OAuth 2.0 for applications requiring delegated access.

For developers creating an internal integration, a Private Integration Token may be appropriate. Public applications serving multiple accounts generally require a different authentication architecture.

A basic API request follows a familiar pattern:

This example illustrates the structure of a contact-creation request. Production implementations should validate the current endpoint schema, manage tokens securely, handle retries and consider using an upsert operation when duplicate prevention is required.

Two production details deserve attention up front. First, treat a 429 rate-limit response as a normal control signal, not an error: back off exponentially, respect any retry-after value, and never retry in a tight loop. Second, prefer upserts keyed on a stable identifier (a normalised phone number or email address) over blind creates, so a retried request cannot spawn a duplicate contact.

The same principles apply when synchronising information from external applications.

For example, an insurance business might receive enquiries through a specialist quoting system. Rather than manually transferring those records, an integration could normalise the information and send it to the CRM.

The key is to treat the API as part of a broader data architecture, not merely as another way to trigger messages.

APIs are useful when an application needs to retrieve or modify information.

Webhooks address a different requirement: knowing when something has happened.

A webhook-driven architecture might look like this:

A configured webhook sends event data to an external endpoint.

The receiving application verifies and processes the event.

This is particularly useful when connecting CRM activity to internal dashboards, fulfilment systems or custom databases.

However, webhook integrations require careful handling.

A publicly accessible webhook endpoint shouldn't automatically trust every request it receives.

Where the platform provides signature verification, developers should validate the signature using the documented method before processing the payload. In practice this means computing an HMAC over the raw request body with the shared signing secret and comparing it to the signature header in constant time — never parsing the body first and re-serialising it, which can change the bytes the signature was computed over.

If an appointment event triggers an invoice, processing the same event twice could create duplicate invoices.

An idempotent handler should recognise an event that has already been processed and avoid repeating side effects. The reliable way to do this is to store each event's unique ID (or a hash of its payload) in a small processed-events table and check it before running any side effect — a duplicate delivery then becomes a cheap no-op instead of a second invoice.

Long-running operations shouldn't necessarily execute inside the initial webhook request.

A more resilient approach is to validate the event, record it, acknowledge receipt and place any substantial work on a processing queue. Return a 2xx response within a second or two so the sender does not retry, then let a worker handle the slow steps. Failed jobs should retry with exponential backoff and, after a set number of attempts, land in a dead-letter queue where a human can inspect them instead of being retried forever.

This helps prevent slow third-party services from disrupting event handling.

Consider a company receiving enquiries through paid advertising, website forms and telephone calls.

The objective is simple: respond quickly, qualify potential customers and book appointments.

A practical CRM workflow could follow several stages.

Incoming data should be converted into a consistent format.

Phone numbers should be standardised, source information should be preserved, and duplicate detection should be considered before creating another contact.

It's also important to retain communication preferences and the relevant consent information.

Not every lead should be distributed randomly.

Assignment logic might consider geographical territory, product category, team availability or an existing customer relationship.

For more complicated organisations, routing decisions may need to be calculated in an external service.

Once the lead has been assigned, the system can initiate an appropriate follow-up.

This might involve notifying a sales representative, sending a permitted acknowledgement or offering an appointment booking link.

The workflow needs to respond to changing circumstances.

If the prospect books an appointment, unnecessary booking reminders should stop.

If a sales representative takes ownership, automated outreach may need to pause.

If the prospect opts out, the system must respect that preference across relevant communication channels.

Finally, the system should record what happened.

Useful measurements include lead response time, appointment booking rate, appointment attendance and conversion rate.

These metrics make it possible to distinguish a workflow that simply runs from one that improves business operations.

Artificial intelligence introduces additional possibilities, particularly around lead qualification, conversation handling and information extraction.

An AI-powered system might analyse an incoming enquiry, identify the customer's requirements and suggest an appropriate next action.

It could also summarise a conversation for a sales representative or help classify enquiries into different service categories.

However, introducing an AI model creates new engineering considerations.

Allowing an AI model to generate unrestricted text is rarely sufficient when that output controls business processes.

A more predictable approach is to require structured responses and validate them before updating CRM records.

The application should check that the response matches the expected schema before performing any action.

Some actions are better handled by deterministic rules.

Communication consent, payment processing, account permissions and important customer record changes shouldn't depend solely on a language model's interpretation.

AI can help classify or summarise information, but critical operations should remain subject to validated rules and appropriate human oversight.

AI assistants can reduce repetitive work, but they should also recognise their limitations.

A prospect asking an unusual technical question or requesting a customised proposal may need to speak with a person.

The best automation systems provide clear escalation paths rather than trying to automate every possible interaction.

GoHighLevel's visual workflow builder makes many routine automations accessible to non-developers.

A basic appointment reminder or lead notification may require little custom development.

The situation changes when several systems need to work together.

For example, a business might need to migrate thousands of existing contacts, integrate custom booking software, synchronise records with an accounting platform and implement specialised lead routing.

These projects require more than familiarity with a visual workflow editor.

They involve data modelling, integration architecture, testing, security and operational reliability.

The decision rule is straightforward: use native workflows for anything the platform already does well, and write custom code only where the platform's model runs out — multi-source routing, external system synchronisation, or reporting that spans several platforms. That distinction matters because unnecessary custom development can make a system harder to maintain, while forcing every process into native workflows can create an equally fragile architecture.

Even technically capable teams can run into problems when implementing CRM integrations.

Large workflows containing dozens of branches are difficult to test and maintain.

Smaller, clearly defined workflows are generally easier to troubleshoot.

An integration that works with ten contacts may behave very differently when processing thousands of records.

Developers should account for rate limits, batch processing, exponential backoff and retry behaviour.

Creating a new contact for every incoming event can quickly damage CRM data quality.

Contact matching, normalisation and carefully selected identifiers help reduce duplication.

Access tokens should never be exposed in client-side JavaScript or committed to public repositories.

Use appropriate secret management and grant integrations only the permissions they require.

Successful execution doesn't necessarily mean the system behaves correctly.

Test scenarios should cover missing information, duplicate events, failed API requests, appointment cancellations, opt-outs and unexpected responses from external services.

Observability matters too. Logs and alerts should help identify which step failed and whether the operation can be retried safely.

Not every automation problem needs an API integration.

A useful decision framework is to start with the simplest reliable implementation.

Standard appointment reminders — Native CRM workflows

Basic lead assignment — Native workflows where supported

External application synchronisation — API or integration middleware

Complex routing across several data sources — Custom logic with CRM integration

AI-assisted lead classification — AI service with validated outputs

Enterprise reporting across multiple platforms — Dedicated reporting or data pipeline

Hybrid architectures are often the most practical.

The CRM manages customer interactions and standard business processes, while specialised services handle requirements that benefit from custom code.

This separation can also make future maintenance more manageable.

CRM automation has become considerably more accessible, but ease of implementation shouldn't be confused with reliability.

A workflow that sends a message after a form submission is easy to demonstrate. A system that handles thousands of contacts, prevents duplication, respects communication preferences, synchronises multiple applications and recovers gracefully from failures requires more careful design.

GoHighLevel provides a useful foundation for combining customer management with automation, particularly when its native capabilities are supported by well-designed integrations.

For developers, the opportunity isn't simply to automate more tasks. It's to design systems that remain understandable, maintainable and reliable as businesses grow.

The strongest implementations aren't necessarily the ones with the most workflows, API connections or AI features.

They're the ones where the right information reaches the right system at the right time — and everything continues working when something inevitably goes wrong.

The U.S. has charged five perpetrators and seized more than $750,000 in crypto tied to the scheme.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The U.S. Department of Justice (DOJ) just announced indictments resulting from Operation Ghostbusters, which investigated widespread student loan fraud. According to the agency, bad actors used stolen identities to sign up for student loans and used AI to complete the application, making it appear that the fake student was attending online classes.

While the schools receive the majority of the proceeds of the fraudulent student loan, the remaining amount is funneled into the bank accounts of the fraudsters, who then use mules to move the money from U.S. bank accounts to overseas conspirators. On the other hand, unwitting victims who have skipped college and instead directly joined the workforce after high school are saddled with student debt for a degree they never even signed up for.

Multiple community colleges and universities across the United States started suspecting fraud in the Federal Student Aid (FAFSA) program in 2021. Estimates suggest the U.S. government lost over $200 million because of this activity, and the U.S. Department of Education Office of the Inspector General referred the case to the FBI Richmond Field Office. Currently, more than 40 open fraud cases involve 26 FBI field offices and 20 U.S. Attorney’s offices. The DOJ called it Operation Ghostbusters, which has already resulted in the indictment of five individuals.

“Today, we are announcing the first results of Operation Ghostbusters — a nationwide effort to stop a major fraud scheme targeting federal student aid, low-income Americans, and the American taxpayer,” Assistant Attorney General Colin M. McDonald of the Justice Department’s National Fraud Enforcement Division said in the statement.

“The Department is charging five defendants and announcing the seizure of more than $750,000 in cryptocurrency tied to this scheme through a network associated with Kenyan nationals. This is the first international seizure of its kind in Kenya. If you steal American identities, if you steal taxpayer dollars, if you exploit programs meant to help students build a future — we will find you. Whether you are in Nebraska, New Jersey, Virginia, or Nairobi, we will pursue you.”

While online fraud has existed since the birth of the internet, AI has let scammers become more sophisticated and target bigger payouts. One example is a scammer who used an AI-generated image of Brad Pitt to scam a 53-year-old woman out of $850,000. However, larger groups also use AI tools for their money-making schemes, such as North Korea, which has reportedly used AI to help its workers get jobs in Western companies and earn money to fund the nation’s coffers. The U.S. is taking steps to shut down student loan fraud by deploying automated fraud detection systems, but the added burden of identification and false positives is negatively affecting the country's most vulnerable students.

France's proposed data levy could raise €10 billion annually from major content providers.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Edit 11/1/2026 4:25am PT: Clarified title to state the bill still has to pass the Senate after being added to the budget.

France has approved a proposed tax that aims to tax the amount of internet data per gigabyte sent to French users in the country, with major companies like Google, Netflix, TikTok, and Meta now in the crosshairs. According to minutes released by the National Assembly, members voted 11-10 in support of the motion on October 30 — which now means the new law has been added to the country’s next budget, despite major opposition from the government side and the finance committee rapporteur. The law still has to pass through the Senate for final approval. The latest amendment, introduced by socialist lawmaker Laurent Lhardit, could see content providers pay €0.055 (roughly $0.06) to the government for every gigabyte of content distributed to French users.

With approximately 154 million users and counting in France, the proposal’s authors say such a tax could generate around €10 billion ($11.2 billion) in annual revenue for the government. “This amendment proposes a tax on digital data flows emitted by content providers, to be borne by the providers themselves. Its revenue is estimated at €10 billion.” Said Laurent Lhardit. The said proposal, though yet to be passed as it must survive further parliamentary scrutiny, aims at companies that generate substantial internet traffic as opposed to taxing their advertising revenue, profits and subscriptions. Its explanatory notes particularly mention Google, Meta, Netflix, Amazon, and content delivery network Akamai as major potential contributors. Notably, TikTok and other similar platforms could fall under this broader scope depending on how these rules are applied.

Internet service providers, including Orange Free and Liad, would be required to measure the volume of data and identify the companies responsible for sending it to French users. The Data will then be collected and submitted quarterly to France’s tax administration under the supervision of telecommunications watchdog Arcep. Aside from the €0.055 per gigabyte levy, the proposal also includes a cap of 15% of a company’s French revenue or 2% of its worldwide revenue. It also accounts for content delivery networks (CDNs), which store copies of online content closer to users to reduce loading times and network congestion.

Several European countries already impose digital service taxes, generally based on revenue from Ads, marketplaces and other digital activities. In March, Meta announced it would introduce location-based fees of between 2% and 55 for advertisers to offset digital services taxes and other government levies in several countries, including France, Italy, and Spain, from July 1, 2026 . Previously, the European Commission has rolled out major enforcement actions against major technology companies, including a €5 billion fine against Google over Android antitrust violations, Facebook’s €100 million fine for misleading European regulators, and the EU’s investigation into Facebook’s ability to link WhatsApp accounts.

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

Last year, Apple and Meta were also fined €500 million and €200 million, respectively, a move that strained the relationship between Brussels and Washington, with the U.S. calling the penalties ‘a novel form of economic extortion’ that it will not tolerate. That said, the amendments' adoption in parliament is only the first step. If it can clear the remaining stages of France’s 2027 budget process, it can become law, with parliament scheduled to vote on the budget’s first section in early November.

The Bambu Lab P1S combo at its discounted price of $479.99 is an excellent entry point for anyone looking to get into 3D printing without compromising on speed, print quality, or multicolor printing capabilities.

More jet-powered RC aircraft projects are aiming to reach Mach 1.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

A new 'Reaper' jet-powered drone project just unofficially broke the previous drone speed world record of 465 mph by hitting 491 mph. Tomas Salvo is an aerospace engineering student at Georgia Tech, and his project was assembled in his dorm room. The drone is comprised of a custom-molded carbon fiber shell, laser-cut internal structure, and a 250N jet engine with a titanium 3D-printed nozzle, costing $13,000 to build. Tomas Salvo aims to win the $800,000 Boom Prize posted by Boom Supersonic CEO Blake Scholl.

My college dorm project just broke the world speed record. Reaper hit 790 km/h (491 mph) carrying a 1.2 kg (2.6 lb) payload. These 3 Reapers are now the world’s fastest small jet-powered drones. Each of them can hold that speed for 4 minutes on a full tank. Reaper can be rail- or truck-launched, climb to 2,000 m (6,600 ft) in under 20 seconds, then fly for 50 km (31 mi) at full speed. This is now a reliable and repeatable system. Future testing and small optimizations should push the top speed past 800 km/h (500 mph).October 30, 2026

Tomas Salvo’s Reaper project, which aimed to beat Niels Herbrich’s world record for the fastest jet-powered RC aircraft, went viral and caught the attention of Boom Supersonic CEO Blake Scholl, who sponsors the $800,000 Boom Prize ($750,000 cash, $50,000 Boom stock). The prize’s conditions are: First, pass Mach 1 for at least 5 seconds while climbing or level, not descending. Second, it must be an air-breathing aircraft with no on-board oxidizers or rockets for propulsion. Third, it must land intact, and lastly, the same aircraft (wings, fuselage, engine, and flight computer) must fly twice in one day. The participants must be individuals or teams, not corporations or people backed by venture capitalists or government funding; they want garage hobbyists.

We recently covered Mach Initiative’s Kingchaser, a 3D-printed jet-powered RC aircraft also aimed at beating Herbrich’s world record. They were beaten by Tomas Salvo, but Tomas’ run was not officially recorded by Guinness. The Kingchaser project was inspired by the Boom Prize; however, since they are not US citizens, they are not eligible to win it, but are still aiming to have an officially recorded Guinness World Record. The Reaper has a smaller engine than the Kingchaser at only 250N, compared to the Kingchaser’s 300N, but achieved a flight speed of 491 mph, beating the Kingchaser’s test flight of only 273 mph, as the latter did not have enough space to accelerate. However, Mach Initiative estimates a top speed of 550 mph if they had the space.

While the Reaper isn’t made out of PETG like the Kingchaser, it still utilizes some 3D printing, albeit in a much more expensive fashion — it has a titanium 3D-printed nozzle for its engine that replaces the stock nozzle, allowing the engine to sit further forward, improving the center of gravity without adding ballast on the front, weighing in at a total of 11 pounds. It’s made out of custom-molded carbon fiber on the outside with a laser-cut internal structure, all designed by Tomas Salvo and assembled in his dorm.

The Reaper was able to achieve its 491 mph speed while carrying a 1.2kg payload, with Tomas’ post further explaining that it could hold the speed for four minutes on a full tank, climb to 6,500 feet in under 20 seconds, and fly for a distance of 31 miles at full speed.

Before the Boom Prize, Tomas was able to land a few sponsorships that got him a JetCat 250N engine; however, we’re not sure how this fits with the Boom Prize’s rules barring corporate sponsorship. Since the Boom Prize's announcement, however, Tomas has resorted to crowdfunding, asking for $50,000 in August after raising $20,000, the source of which he did not disclose.

Both the Kingchaser and the Reaper are aiming at Herbrich’s world record, but they are still far from the 1,234.8 km/h or 767.3 mph Mach 1 speed that they need to surpass in order to win the Boom Prize. Mach Initiative responded to Blake Scholl’s X post about the Boom Prize, saying “challenge accepted,” but the rules may make them ineligible for the prize, making the Reaper a real contender for the $800,000, as it currently sits unrivaled by any other US project.

#10260. How to Create Effective Cold Email Sequences That Generate Replies: A Developer's Guide

Have 30-200 Employees? Make $50k-$500k selling your data for AI Training.

Have 30-200 Employees? Make $50k-$500k selling your data for AI Training.

Stay up to date with AI tools, model releases, and developer workflows that matter.

Connecting with prospects via cold email isn't always straightforward, particularly when recipients receive dozens of messages every day. Even a relevant email can get lost in a crowded inbox. That's why many B2B companies use cold email sequences to maintain consistent communication and manage follow-ups.

However, creating effective email sequences involves more than writing messages and choosing sending intervals. Developers building or integrating email automation systems must also consider API reliability, message scheduling, data validation, email authentication, security, and workflow monitoring.

A well-designed email sequence combines relevant communication with reliable technical infrastructure. This article explains how to structure cold email sequences and explores the development practices that make automated email workflows more dependable.

A cold email sequence is a planned series of emails sent to prospective customers who have not previously established a direct relationship with the sender. These messages are delivered over a defined period, with each email designed to introduce an offer, provide additional context, address a potential concern, or encourage a response.

For example, a software development company might contact a growing SaaS business to discuss opportunities to improve its application infrastructure. The first email could introduce the company's expertise, the second could explain a relevant technical challenge, and the third could share a useful observation about application performance.

Each message supports the same overall objective while contributing something different.

From a developer's perspective, a cold email sequence can also be understood as an event-driven workflow. Every contact has a sequence state, scheduled messages, and conditions that determine whether the next message should be delivered.

A basic sequence might follow this structure:

This model is useful for developers working on CRM integrations, email platforms, customer engagement systems, and automated business workflows.

The first step is to establish a clear plan before writing any messages. Determine who you want to contact, what problem your offer solves, and what action you want recipients to take.

Start by defining your ideal customer profile, identifying relevant decision-makers, and researching their business needs. Next, select one primary campaign objective, such as generating positive replies, booking qualified meetings, or exploring potential partnerships.

Decide how many emails you will send, what each message will communicate, and how much time will pass between follow-ups. Make sure every email adds value instead of repeating information from previous messages.

For example, a software consultancy targeting B2B SaaS companies might introduce an application performance challenge in the first email, share a practical recommendation in the second, provide a relevant engineering case study in the third, and close the sequence with a polite final follow-up.

Before launching the campaign, review the prospect list, verify contact information, check email authentication, and test the complete sequence.

Developers should also establish technical requirements before implementation:

Define how contact information will be stored and validated.

Determine which events trigger scheduled emails.

Implement conditions that prevent unnecessary follow-ups.

Establish secure API connections between applications.

Record delivery results, failures, and recipient responses.

These considerations help transform a basic email campaign into a maintainable automation workflow.

Every cold email sequence needs a specific objective. Without one, messages may contain several unrelated offers or ask recipients to take different actions.

A SaaS company might want to schedule product demonstrations, while a software consultancy may want to connect with businesses planning infrastructure improvements. A recruitment firm might aim to establish relationships with technical hiring managers.

Your objective should influence the message and call to action.

For developers building campaign dashboards, goals should also be represented through measurable application events.

This example demonstrates how a dashboard might calculate a positive reply rate from stored campaign events.

In production, developers should also handle zero denominators, duplicate events, inconsistent reporting periods, and differences between accepted, delivered, and failed messages.

Effective cold email sequences begin with the right audience. Even a well-written email is unlikely to generate meaningful replies if it reaches people who have no interest in the subject.

Start by defining your ideal customer profile. Consider industry, company size, location, business model, growth stage, and the challenges your product or service addresses.

For example, a development consultancy offering cloud migration assistance might contact CTOs, engineering managers, and technical founders. Although these professionals may benefit from similar services, their priorities can differ.

A CTO may focus on infrastructure reliability and operational costs, while an engineering manager may prioritize deployment workflows, maintainability, and team productivity.

Segment prospects according to meaningful differences rather than sending identical messages to everyone.

Audience segmentation can be implemented through database queries, CRM filters, or API-based contact management.

A simplified contact object might look like this:

Structured data makes segmentation more predictable and helps prevent inconsistent personalization.

Contact data should be collected and processed lawfully, with appropriate access controls and retention policies.

Personalization helps prospects understand why you are contacting them specifically. However, effective personalization involves more than inserting a first name, company name, or job title into a template.

Research relevant professional information that connects the recipient's business to your offer.

For example, a development consultancy contacting a SaaS company could mention a recently announced application launch and explain how scalable deployment practices may support future growth.

The observation should be accurate and connected to a genuine technical challenge.

A practical personalization process involves three steps:

Identify a relevant professional observation.

Explain why it may matter to the recipient.

Connect it naturally to the proposed solution.

Developers can support this process with reusable templates and structured variables.

This simple example demonstrates template personalization.

In production, developers should validate template variables, prevent accidental disclosure of customer data, and escape untrusted values when generating HTML.

The subject line gives recipients an initial indication of what an email contains. A clear, relevant subject line helps them recognize the message's purpose.

Keep subject lines concise and connected to the recipient's business needs. Avoid exaggerated promises, artificial urgency, and misleading claims.

A question about your development workflow

Ideas for improving deployment reliability

A question about your engineering process

The best subject line depends on the audience and purpose.

Developers building email systems should also consider subject-line encoding, Unicode handling, template validation, and protection against header injection.

For example, email libraries should be responsible for constructing message headers rather than applications manually concatenating untrusted header strings.

SitePoint's tutorial on sending email using Node.js provides additional guidance on email construction and delivery.

The first email introduces your business and gives the prospect a reason to consider your offer.

A useful structure includes a personalized opening, a clear value proposition, a brief explanation of relevant expertise, and one simple call to action.

For example, a development consultancy contacting a SaaS business might explain that it helps software teams improve deployment workflows and application reliability.

Avoid introducing every service you offer or listing multiple benefits in the same email.

From a technical perspective, the first message should also establish the recipient's sequence state.

This state information allows a background worker to determine when an email is eligible for delivery.

Before sending, the worker must recheck the latest recipient status. A scheduled job should never override a newly recorded opt-out.

Subject: A question about your development workflow

I noticed [relevant observation about the company's software development process] and thought there might be an opportunity to improve deployment reliability.

Our team works with growing software companies on application infrastructure, development workflows, and performance improvements.

Is improving your development infrastructure a priority for your team this quarter?

The personalized observation should be specific and accurate. A concise message is more useful than a generic introduction filled with broad claims.

An effective cold email sequence follows a logical progression. Each message builds on the initial contact by introducing new information, clarifying value, or offering another way to engage.

Email sequencing tools can help teams schedule messages and manage follow-ups consistently. For developers integrating these systems into existing applications, it is important to understand how scheduling, contact states, reply events, and automation rules interact.

A five-email sequence can provide a practical starting point:

These intervals are starting points rather than universal rules. Adjust them to suit the audience, recipient preferences, and campaign performance.

For developers, scheduling is one of the most important technical aspects of email automation.

Sending messages directly inside an HTTP request can create unnecessary delays and make failure handling difficult.

A more reliable architecture uses background jobs.

This approach separates campaign management from email delivery.

For example, a worker can process scheduled messages independently while the main application continues handling user requests.

SitePoint's guide to implementing a task queue in Node.js explains how background processing can support workloads such as email delivery.

Production implementations should include persistent scheduling, retry policies, rate limiting, and idempotency controls to reduce duplicate messages.

Follow-up emails give prospects another opportunity to respond when they overlook an initial message or need additional information.

Each follow-up should introduce a useful detail, clarify the offer, or address a relevant concern.

For example, a software consultancy could use its first follow-up to explain a common deployment bottleneck. A later message might share a relevant case study or describe how the consultancy approaches application monitoring.

Subject: Re: A question about your development workflow

I wanted to follow up on my previous message.

One area worth reviewing is whether your deployment process includes automated testing, rollback procedures, and application performance monitoring.

These practices can help development teams identify problems earlier and improve release reliability.

Would a brief conversation about your current development workflow be useful?

This follow-up adds context without repeating the original message.

Stop the sequence when someone replies, opts out, or otherwise indicates that further contact is unwelcome.

Prospects receiving unsolicited emails may have little reason to trust an unfamiliar sender.

Relevant evidence helps establish credibility.

Social proof can include verified customer results, technical case studies, industry experience, or examples of previous work.

For example, a software consultancy might share a documented case study showing how automated testing reduced deployment failures.

When case studies are unavailable, demonstrate expertise through a practical observation or useful recommendation.

Developers building campaign systems should ensure that reusable case studies and message templates are versioned appropriately. This makes it easier to identify which content was sent and prevents outdated claims from being reused unintentionally.

Avoid exaggerated claims, invented statistics, and guarantees that cannot be supported.

A call to action tells the recipient what to do next.

In cold email outreach, a complicated request can discourage engagement because the prospect has not yet developed enough interest to commit time or resources.

Is this a priority for your team right now?

Would a short technical overview be useful?

Are you the right person to discuss this with?

Would you be open to exploring a potential partnership?

Choose a CTA that matches the purpose of the email.

Developers designing email templates should also consider accessibility. Links should use descriptive text, buttons should have sufficient contrast, and HTML emails should include readable plain-text alternatives.

Beyond content and scheduling, developers need to ensure that automated email workflows operate securely and consistently.

Email authentication helps receiving systems evaluate whether a message is authorized to use its sending domain.

SPF (Sender Policy Framework): Identifies servers authorized to send mail for a domain.

DKIM (DomainKeys Identified Mail): Uses cryptographic signatures to help verify message integrity and signing-domain responsibility.

DMARC (Domain-based Message Authentication, Reporting, and Conformance): Defines how SPF or DKIM authentication should align with the visible From domain and provides reporting capabilities.

Developers responsible for email infrastructure should understand how these mechanisms work together.

SitePoint's article on email deliverability provides additional context about authentication, sender reputation, and delivery reliability.

Modern email workflows often integrate CRM systems, databases, automation platforms, and email service providers.

REST APIs allow developers to exchange information between these applications.

For example, an application might synchronize contact records, create campaigns, retrieve reply events, or update sequence states.

When building integrations, developers should:

Avoid exposing sensitive information in logs.

These practices improve maintainability and reduce the risk of inconsistent campaign data.

Webhooks allow external platforms to notify applications when events occur.

A simplified event handler could look like this:

This example illustrates application-level event handling rather than a particular provider's webhook format.

A production webhook endpoint must verify the provider's authentication or signature mechanism before processing events. It should also handle duplicate deliveries, out-of-order events, and temporary database failures.

Testing is essential when an application sends automated messages.

Messages are scheduled at the correct times.

Failed jobs do not create duplicate messages.

Templates render correctly across email clients.

API failures produce useful operational logs.

Tools such as Postman, Mailpit, and automated integration tests can help validate different parts of the workflow.

Use synthetic recipients and controlled test environments rather than sending test campaigns to real prospects.

Developers building campaign dashboards or CRM-connected applications may need to integrate an external sequencing platform rather than implement every sending feature themselves.

EmailBison is one example of a platform for managing cold email campaigns and follow-ups. Its API and integration capabilities make it relevant when evaluating how a third-party email system can fit into an existing application architecture.

For developers, the main consideration is not simply whether a platform can schedule messages, but how its capabilities interact with the application's data model and operational requirements.

For example, a CRM integration might follow this workflow:

This architecture introduces several engineering considerations.

Data synchronization: Contact records must remain consistent between the CRM and the email platform.

Error handling: API failures should be recorded and retried safely when appropriate.

Security: API credentials and contact information should be protected.

Observability: Developers should be able to investigate failed operations without exposing sensitive information.

The same architectural principles apply to other email automation providers. Before choosing an integration, review its current API documentation, authentication model, event coverage, rate limits, and data-handling requirements.

Several common mistakes can reduce the effectiveness and reliability of cold email outreach, even when businesses use automation.

Sending generic messages: Emails that could apply to any business fail to establish relevance. Use appropriate segmentation and accurate personalization.

Repeating the same pitch: Every follow-up should contribute something new instead of repeating the original message.

Making emails too long: Excessive explanations and multiple offers make it harder for prospects to identify the main point.

Using aggressive follow-ups: Sending messages too frequently can frustrate recipients. Choose reasonable intervals and respect recipient preferences.

Making unsupported claims: Exaggerated results and misleading promises damage credibility.

Ignoring recipient responses: Stop automated sequences when someone replies, opts out, or requests no further contact.

Failing to handle duplicate events: A webhook may be delivered more than once. Applications should use idempotent processing to avoid repeated actions.

Ignoring API failures: Temporary provider outages and rate limits should not silently corrupt campaign state.

Insufficient testing: Automation workflows should be validated with controlled test data before production deployment.

Scaling too quickly: Increasing sending volume before validating deliverability, infrastructure, and workflow reliability can amplify existing problems.

Avoiding these mistakes helps teams maintain a professional communication process while reducing operational risks.

A cold email sequence can start with three to five emails, including the initial message and follow-ups. The appropriate number depends on the audience, purpose, and recipient preferences.

Focus on providing useful information rather than increasing the number of messages unnecessarily.

Yes. Developers can integrate email sequencing platforms with web applications through APIs, webhooks, and background workers.

Common use cases include CRM synchronization, campaign dashboards, reply tracking, and workflow automation.

A practical starting point is two to four days before the first follow-up, followed by slightly longer intervals between later messages.

The schedule should account for recipient preferences and stop immediately when further contact is unwanted.

Yes. Sequencing tools can automate scheduling, follow-ups, contact management, and reply detection.

Human oversight remains important for message quality, data accuracy, security, and responding appropriately to interested prospects.

Testing should also verify that automated messages are not sent to suppressed contacts.

Yes. Small businesses may prioritize straightforward communication and immediate practical benefits, while enterprise prospects may require more information about security, integration, scalability, and stakeholder approval.

From a development perspective, enterprise integrations may also require stronger access controls, audit logging, and data synchronization.

Creating effective cold email sequences requires relevant messaging, thoughtful follow-ups, and reliable automation.

For developers, the underlying technical systems are just as important as the messages themselves. Contact validation, background scheduling, API integrations, email authentication, webhook processing, and error handling all contribute to dependable email workflows.

By treating email sequencing as a structured, event-driven application process rather than simply a series of scheduled messages, development teams can build systems that are easier to test, monitor, maintain, and extend.

The goal is not merely to send more emails. It is to create a reliable communication workflow that respects recipients and supports meaningful conversations.

#10259. Google stock drops amid AI fears; AI Mode has ‘lower monetization,’ getting more ads

Google is bringing ads to AI Mode globally as it admits that the new take on Search brings “lower monetization,” all while $GOOG stock is seeing a fairly major drop.

Alphabet’s Q3 earnings call yesterday saw Google’s Philipp Schindler acknowledging that AI Mode, the company’s Gemini-powered new take on Search, has “lower monetization” compared to traditional Search. So the “strong growth” of AI Mode didn’t have as much of a positive impact on Google’s bottom line simply because the mode doesn’t generate as much revenue.

…It’s worth noting that the strong growth in AI Mode queries had an impact because of the lower monetization we’re seeing in AI Mode. To be clear, we feel very good about our trajectory here. Our ad experiments have shown promising results and we’re now scaling ads within both AI Overviews and AI Mode to more users and more queries…

In a blog post the same day, Google announced that AI Mode would be getting ads in global markets. US users will also start to see “multiturn ads” in follow-up queries.

Our research shows that people are comfortable with ads in AI Mode and find them highly relevant. In fact, click-through rates are already similar between sponsored and organic product list items within AI Mode.

Now we’re scaling ads in AI Mode in Australia, Canada, India, Kenya, Malaysia, New Zealand, Nigeria, Pakistan, the Philippines, and Singapore. In Q4 2026, we plan to expand to additional locales, including Brazil, Mexico, and the United Kingdom.

Meanwhile, $GOOG stock dipped by 27 points around the time of the earnings call and has continued to drop on October 29. It currently sits around $280, just a few points higher than a March 30 low of $273.14. The 12% drop reduces Google’s market cap by over $500 billion. NBC News points out that it is the second-biggest drop behind Nvidia’s $589 billion drop in 2025.

The drop is not directly due to AI Mode itself, but is attributed to weaker-than-expected earnings in Q3. Ad revenue from Search still makes up roughly half of Google’s overall revenue.

Our Daniel Bader expands on this in the latest issue of Inbox, our twice-weekly free newsletter:

…But investors are looking at the capital expenditure tied up in future data center buildouts and getting nervous that, if the bubble bursts, even these well-capitalized companies will take massive hits.

The biggest question surrounding Google’s stock performance is whether it can sufficiently monetize AI-powered search before demand for its traditional search inventory falls off a cliff. As the company sends less and less traffic to the open web, it’s touting the fact that AI Mode and AI Overviews keep people on Google for three times as long as traditional search.

The U.S. is developing the Rocket Cargo capability for the entire military.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Air Force Research Lab (AFRL), the research and development arm of the US Air Force, has successfully tested Rocket Cargo capabilities, which would allow military units to resupply via cargo pods dropped from space. According to the AFRL’s X post, the cargo pods, which were onboard a SpaceX Starfall capsule, launched from the Cape Canaveral Space Force Station as part of the Bandwagon-5 “rideshare” mission carried by a Falcon 9 rocket. The capsule splashed down in the Pacific Ocean for this test, with the unit containing mission equipment like MREs, bottled water, a SATCOM-enabled tracker, spare batteries, and other equipment. The AFRL also fit-checked a UNI-PAC III life raft to see if it would fit inside the Starfall capsule for a potential test in the future.

The mission is a test of Rocket Cargo’s point-to-point delivery (P2D) capabilities, which should allow forces to resupply virtually anywhere in the world without needing to risk other assets like aircraft and aircrews, especially in areas where there is active combat.

With support from @SpaceX, the AFRL agile cargo pods were recovered after splashdown in the Pacific Ocean. Each pod contained representative mission equipment to study the effects of temperature, shock, and vibration of the cargo environments during launch and reentry. #Readiness https://t.co/yWoND13ur4October 26, 2026

“This was a critical achievement to demonstrate the potential of Rocket Cargo delivery for point-to-point rapid global mobility for the Future Force," AFRL Technology Transition Office director Adam Remaly said in a statement. "The cross-discipline collaborations and ability to unleash the speed of our defense industrial base are key to maximizing capability and winning the future.”

The Starfall capsule, which looks like a hockey puck, measures about 10 feet across and has a capacity of up to 2,200 pounds. It uses a carbon fiber heat shield to protect its contents during reentry and uses compressed gas thrusters for attitude control in flight. Finally, it deploys a parachute system to slow down the entire system for landing. While 2,200 pounds might already seem like a substantial capacity, the Air Force is reportedly hoping that it could increase the limit in the order of tens of tons, potentially allowing it to resupply ammunition, weapon systems, and even deploy vehicles from space. This would have to wait for the larger SpaceX Starship rockets, which are still in development, though.

Even though the latest test seemingly relies mostly on SpaceX technologies, it’s not the only company developing that capability for the U.S. military. Sierra Space, a subsidiary of the Sierra Nevada Corporation, is also working on the Ghost reentry vehicle and has already conducted test drops of the system from heights of 35,000 and 68,500 feet.

The capability to resupply troops across long distances without needing to rely on a long logistical tail is crucial for the U.S., especially as it faces a potential adversary in the Pacific. It’s also for this reason that the U.S. Navy has been experimenting with 3D-printing technologies, allowing it to print parts onboard carriers and at forward bases instead of waiting for shipments from across the ocean.

#10256. Google Home update should make Gemini stop saying ‘Sorry, I didn’t understand’ so much

Google’s latest Gemini for Home update targets a few issues, like one where speakers randomly blurt out “I didn’t understand” when no one asks.

The second October Gemini for Home update was released by Google today. It bundles a few fixes with a lengthy section in the changelog dedicated to the Google Home app.

I would probably tell Gemini, "Here, do you understand this?" as I threw it out the window

But there are six individual changes for Gemini within the Home app. One fix stands out the most, though. According to the update post, Google was aware that Home and Nest speakers/displays were facing a bug that would prompt them to say “Sorry, I didn’t understand” at random. That would happen unprompted, which is understandably a little unsettling. That strange bug should be fixed in this new update.

Further down, two fixes target Gemini’s ability to understand users on Google Home devices. For one, it would misunderstand requests to move music from one room to another. In other cases, it would incorrectly adjust sleep, media, or normal timers instead of the requested type. Both of those features will reportedly work as intended going forward.

The changelog includes a couple of other adjustments, which should make for an overall less stressful experience.

Updates should be live for users starting today, though it might take some time for them to roll out to everyone. Hopefully, Gemini will only let you know it doesn’t understand if it genuinely doesn’t understand.

#10255. Voyager 1 probe to hit historic one light-day distance from Earth in 17 days — 49-year-old spacecraft travels 16 billion miles

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Voyager 1 spacecraft, which was designed for a five-year mission to observe Jupiter and Saturn, is about to reach a distance of one light-day from Earth after nearly 50 years in flight. According to NASA, the space probe will get to that marker on Nov. 18, 2026, at 5:16:07 a.m. EST, making it the most distant artificial object in history. The agency has now posted a countdown clock to mark the historic moment it becomes the first human-made object to reach that distance.

One light-day isn’t a span of time, but the distance it takes for light to travel in 24 hours. Since light travels at around 186,282 miles per second or 300,000 km per second, that’s a distance of about 16 billion miles or 26 billion kilometers. By comparison, the Moon is 238,855 miles (384,400 kilometers) from Earth, while our planet is 93 million miles (149.6 million kilometers) from the Sun.

Voyager 1’s estimated speed in relation to the Sun is 37,844.49 mph, which makes it pretty fast (but still not the fastest human-made object ever built — the Parker Solar Probe’s holds the record at 394,736 mph). Despite that, it will still take the spacecraft 49 years, 2 months, and 13 days to get to one light-day from Earth.

This is actually one of Voyager 1’s achievements, as NASA scientists have extended its lifespan by nearly ten times the length of its original mission timeline. It also hasn’t been easy for the space probe — in 2024, it suffered from a critical memory error, which engineers were able to fix after weeks of diagnosis. The distance has made repairing the spacecraft quite a challenge, as the latency at that time was already more than 22 hours, with round-trip communications taking at least 45 hours. Earlier this year, NASA had to use an emergency instrument shutdown procedure after an unexpected drop in power levels.

Experts estimate that the three radioisotope thermoelectric generators (RTGs) that power the spacecraft have enough juice in them to allow Voyager 1 to continue operating until the 2030s. So, unless it encounters a major issue, we can expect it to continue sending data back to Earth as it continues to travel through interstellar space. As the space probe continues its journey, its heading is set towards a star called AC +79 3888 or Gliese 445. This heavenly body is located within the Ursa Minor constellation, with the ship expected to pass within 1.7 light-years of the star in 17 light-years. But given that Voyager 1 only travels at 0.00564325% of the speed of light, we estimate that it will take the ship more than 300,000 years to reach its next destination.

These autonomous drones could deliver over 500 gallons of firefighting foam to a target area.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The San Bernardino County Fire Protection District has just acquired a fleet of five autonomous drones that will allow it to battle wildfires before they become worse. According to a press release (via PR Newswire), the drones will be provided by Seneca Systems, which will deploy the drones and a mobile operations base. These units also come with autonomy software, allowing a single pilot to supervise the entire team, which is capable of delivering over 500 gallons of firefighting foam to a target area.

BREAKING: The largest county in America by land area is deploying autonomous drones to fight wildfires. @Seneca_Systems has secured a deal with San Bernardino County Fire to deploy five autonomous firefighting drones, a mobile operations base, and autonomy software designed to rapidly suppress newly detected wildfires before they spread. The five-drone strike team can deliver over 500 gallons of finished firefighting foam, with a single pilot supervising multiple aircraft. The system is designed for rapid response, including nighttime operations and difficult-to-access terrain. San Bernardino County Fire Chief Dan Munsey has set an ambitious goal to stop 90% of fires before they grow beyond 10 square feet, with Seneca's autonomous rapid initial attack capabilities potentially playing a key role. The drones are expected to enter service ahead of the 2027 wildfire season.October 30, 2026

San Bernardino is the largest county in California and the United States by land area. Given the fact that the state is prone to wildfires, especially during spring, summer, and early fall, autonomous firefighting drones would help its firefighters cover more area and address localized fires before they become larger threats. “Our goal is to stop 90% of fires before they grow larger than 10 square feet,” San Bernardino County Fire Protection District chief Dan Munsey said.

"Seneca's aircraft give us a capability that has never before been possible: autonomous rapid initial attack. We've been working closely with Seneca for over a year to develop a system that meets our needs. This product will help us to get more quickly to parts of the County that are at the highest risk and help make those communities safer. It force-multiplies what our firefighters can do, and it keeps them safer while they do it."

The five-drone strike could be launched from anywhere, and they’re capable of reaching starting fires in under 10 minutes of detection. More importantly, the drones’ onboard sensor suite allows firefighters to operate them 24/7, allowing them to get to hard-to-reach areas even at night. This should help Munsey achieve his target of putting out wildfires before they become larger, difficult to control, and a threat to the county’s two million residents. More importantly, giving one firefighter the ability to direct five units could potentially free up other firefighting crews to respond to other incidents.

Civilian first-responder agencies have been using drones to augment their operations for years, but this is one of the first instances that an AI-powered Seneca drone will be deployed to support their operations. Nevertheless, Drones-as-First-Responder (DFR) systems have increasingly become more popular in the U.S., with both startups, like BRINC, and established companies, such as Flock, offering units to cut 911 response times. We’ve even seen some states deploy “Campus Guardian Angel” drones to help neutralize school shooters.

#10250. Reddit will soon be a ‘logged-in-only’ website, forcing you to have an account

Reddit is shifting to a “logged-in-only experience,” the company confirmed during its latest earnings call.

While the timeline is vague at this point, Reddit has confirmed that it will be locking down the site for users without an account, also impacting usage in private browsing modes. CEO Steve Huffman explains in a shareholder letter and a post on Reddit that the website will becoming a “logged-in-only experience.”

Moving to primarily logged-in will ensure our users have the best Reddit experience possible, and can find the content and communities most relevant to them. It is better for our business because we will have more signals for ads targeting, and being logged-in leads to higher engagement generally. It also allows us to protect access to Reddit’s extremely valuable corpus of data, and helps us better adapt to emerging regulations around the world.

In Q4 we’ll begin fully testing this in select markets outside the U.S. The results of those tests will inform an optimized version of logged-in-only Reddit globally.

The change also comes as Reddit says that search referral traffic was “down meaningfully” in Q3 2026, mainly in regards to logged-out traffic. “Down meaningfully” was later detailed to mean that traffic “dropped by more than 1 million referrals on average per week.”

"It also allows us to protect access to Reddit’s extremely valuable corpus of data" What he means is they want to prevent access to Reddit's data, unless they get paid for it. What has always annoyed me about Reddit is that they think it is their data. Almost everything on the site is generated by users, it's their data, not Reddit's.

As for private browsing, Huffman says that Reddit has “rebuilt and [is] re-releasing incognito mode in our app.”

Details on what will and will not be available to logged-out users are unclear at this time, though Huffman says that “the front page being available logged-out is useful and important,” adding that “visitors arriving from search or other external sources to see the content that’s being linked to.” He further implies that AI scraping is certainly a major part of this decision, saying that “the vast majority of unauthorized access to Reddit (basically the scraping and stealing of Reddit’s data) happens through our logged-out experiences.”

This comes shortly after Reddit rolled out a new personalized “For You” homepage.
